In Zech. 9, it is not only foretold, v. 1, that "the eyes of man should be towards the Lord, as all the tribes of Israel," but in v. 7, 'tis represented as though the Gentiles, even of the Philistines, should be accounted as belonging to Israel. 'Tis said, Ekron shall be "as a Jebusite"; but the Jebusites, who were hewers of wood and drawers of water for the house of God, were reputed, according to the Law of Moses, as some of God's covenant people, of the men of Israel, and of the posterity of Abraham, Isaac and Jacob. Deut. 29:10-13, "Ye stand this day all of you before the Lord your God; your captains of your tribes, your elders, and your officers, *with all the men of Israel*, your little ones, your wives, and *thy stranger* that is in the camp, *from the hewer of thy wood unto the drawer of thy water*: that thou shouldst enter into a covenant with the Lord thy God, and into his oath, which the Lord thy God maketh with thee this day: that he may establish thee today for a people unto himself, [. . .] as he hath said unto thee, and as he hath sworn unto *thy fathers, to Abraham, to Isaac, and to Jacob*." Yea, in this place in Zechariah, it is not only foretold that some of them should belong to God's people Israel, but that some of them should be advanced to chief places of authority and honor among them: "he shall be as a governor in Judah, and Ekron as a Jebusite" [Zech. 9:7].

And God does expressly call the Gentile converts, "the children of Israel," in Hos. 1:9-10. "Then said God, Call his name Lo-ammi: for ye are not my people, and I will not be your God. Yet the number of *the children of Israel* shall be as the sand of the sea."

The Gentiles are called "the people of the God of Abraham," Ps. 47:9. See SSS.[^007-note8]

"The Talmudists affirm, that by Jerusalem" in the last chapters of Ezekiel "we are to understand the gathering of the Gentiles to Christ, or the whole body of Christians." Greenhill on Ezekiel, vol. 2, p. 604.[^007-note9]

147.[^007-note10] It was foretold that the far greater part of the people of God, should be of the Gentiles in the times of the Messiah's kingdom. Is. 54:1-2, "Sing, O barren, and thou that didst not bear; break forth into singing, cry aloud, thou that didst not travail with child; for more are the children of the desolate than the children of the married wife, saith the Lord. Enlarge the place of thy tent, let them stretch forth the curtains of thine habitations: spare not, lengthen thy cords, strengthen thy stakes"; Is. 52:15, "So shall he sprinkle many nations." See the next verse [Is. 53:1], concerning the Jews, "Who hath believed our report?" So, as has been observed before, the prophecies speak of but a small remnant of the Jewish nation that should adhere to the Messiah, but an innumerable multitude of Gentiles are often spoken of as flowing together to the goodness, to God's holy mountain, and the like; and what was done as to the Jews, is spoken of as a light thing in comparison of the multitude of Gentiles that should be saved. Is. 49:6, "It is a light thing that thou shouldst be my servant to raise up the tribes of Jacob, and restore the preserved of Israel: I will also give thee for a light of the Gentiles, that thou mayst be my salvation to the ends of the earth"; Hos. 1:9-10, "ye are not my people, . . . Yet the children of Israel shall be as the sand of the sea, which cannot be measured nor numbered." The people of God of the Gentiles are represented as ten to one of the Jews, in Zech. 8:23. "Then men shall take hold out of all languages of the nations, . . . of the skirt of him that is a Jew," etc.[^007-note11]

148.[^007-note12] That many of the Christian Gentiles do become ecclesiastical officers and ministers in the house of God, is agreeable to Is. 66:21, "And I will take of them for priests and for Levites."

149.[^007-note13] It was foretold that in the times of the Messiah's kingdom, the people or church of God should be called by a new name, and not by the same name that the people of God were wont to be called before the coming of the Messiah and the rejection of the greater part of the Jewish nation. Is. 62:2, "And the Gentiles shall see thy righteousness, and all kings thy glory: and thou shalt be called by a new name, which the mouth of the Lord shall name"; Is. 65:15, "for the Lord God shall slay thee, and call his servants by another name."

This is come to pass, in that the people of God who of old were called by the name of their father Jacob, and so were named Israel, are now called by the name of Jesus Christ, as the church of Christ, and Christians; which last name seems to be given by the direction of Barnabas and Paul and other inspired persons at Antioch, under the conduct of divine inspiration, Acts 11:26. The same Spirit seems to have given the name, that has recorded the giving of it; and so the mouth of the Lord named it.

150.[^007-note14] It was foretold that after this, the name of the Jews, which used to be honorable, should among God's people become odious and esteemed accursed--Is. 65:15, "And you shall leave your name for a curse unto my chosen"--as it has come to pass in the church of Jesus Christ.

151.[^007-note15] It was foretold that in the Messiah's times, there should be given a new divine constitution, diverse from the law or covenant God established with Israel by Moses. This seems to be implied in the Messiah's being a prophet like unto Moses, agreeable to the prophecy, Deut. 18. Is. 2:3, "out of Zion shall go forth the law" (as once it had done from Sinai), "and the word of the Lord from Jerusalem. And he shall judge among the nations." That the Holy Spirit has here a reference to the law's once going forth out of Sinai, is confirmed by v. 5 following, where seems to [be] pointed forth a great difference in the manners of its going forth, and the great privilege God's people should have by his appearing in glory as he should do at Mt. Sion, beyond what Israel had when they saw God's glory at Mt. Sinai. There, God's glory was so terrible that the children of Israel could not endure to behold it; but here, it is so mild and sweet that God's people with great pleasure walk in the light of it. "O house of Jacob, come, let us walk in the light of the Lord." See also Mic. 4:2; Is. 42:4, "the isles shall wait for his law"; v. 6, "I [. . .] will [. . .] give thee for a covenant of the people, for a light of the Gentiles"; Is. 49:8, "I will give thee for a covenant of the people, to establish the earth"; Is. 51:4, "Hearken unto me, my people; and give ear unto me, O my nation: for a law shall proceed from me, and I will make my judgment to rest for a light of the people"; Jer. 31:31-32, "Behold, the days come, saith the Lord, that I will make a new covenant with the house of Israel, and with the house of Judah: not according to the covenant that I made with their fathers in the day that I took them by the hand to bring them out of the land of Egypt"; Ezek. 16:61-62, "and I will give them unto thee" (speaking of Sodom and Samaria) "but not by thy covenant. And I will establish my covenant with thee"; Ezek. 37:26, "Moreover I will make a covenant of peace with them; it shall be an everlasting covenant."

This is also evident in the account we have of a diverse constitution, in the nine last chapters of Ezekiel.[^007-note16]

Is. 65:15-18, "the Lord thy God shall slay thee, and call his servants by another name: . . . because the former troubles are forgotten, and because they are hid from mine eyes. For I create new heavens and a new earth: and the former shall not be remembered, nor come into mind. But be you glad and rejoice for ever in that which I create: for, behold, I create Jerusalem a rejoicing, and her people a joy."

152.[^007-note17] There are many things in the ancient prophecies that do show, that in the times of the Messiah's kingdom, the ceremonial law should be abolished. See "Miscellanies," [nos.] 599, and 1027, 1034, 1040. See SSS on Deut. 10:16.[^007-note18]

Particularly it is prophesied that in those times there should be no ARK and no temple. Jer. 3:16-17, "And it shall come to pass, when ye be multiplied and increased in the land, in those days, saith the Lord, they shall say no more, The ark of the covenant of the Lord: neither shall it come into mind: [. . .] neither shall they visit it; neither shall that be done any more. At that time they shall call Jerusalem the throne of the Lord; and all the nations shall be gathered into it, to the name of the Lord in Jerusalem"; Is. 66:1-2, "Thus saith the Lord, The heaven is my throne, and the earth is my footstool: where is the house that ye build unto me? and where is the place of my rest? For all those things hath my hand made, and all these things have been, saith the Lord: but to this man will I look, even to him that is poor and of a contrite spirit, and trembleth at my work"; Mal. 1:10-11, "I have no pleasure in you, saith the Lord, neither will I accept an offering at your hand. For from the rising of the sun to the going down of the same my name shall be great among the Gentiles; and *in every place* incense shall be offered unto my name, and a pure offering: for my name shall be great among the heathen, saith the Lord of hosts." These things do necessarily imply the abolition of the ceremonial law: for most of the rites and services of the law have respect to the temple and ark, and a dependence thereon. Zeph. 2:11, "and men shall worship him, every one from his place."

It was foretold that eunuchs and strangers should be admitted to the same privileges in God's house with others, Is. 56. But this can't be as long as the ceremonial law of Moses stands in force.

It was foretold that God would take of the Gentiles to be priests and Levites (Is. 66:21), which cannot be in a consistence with the ceremonial law of Moses.

It was foretold that God's great works in bringing up the children of Israel out of Egypt, and carrying them through the Red Sea and wilderness, and bringing them into Canaan, should no more be remembered in the days of the Messiah. Jer. 16:14-15, "Therefore, behold, the days come, saith the Lord, that it shall no more be said, The Lord liveth, that brought up the children of Israel out of the land of Egypt; but, The Lord liveth," etc.; and we have the same words again, Jer. 23:7-8; Is. 43:16-19, "Thus saith the Lord, which maketh a way in the sea, and a path in the mighty waters; which bringeth forth the chariot and horse, the army and the power; and they shall lie down together, [. . .] they are extinct, they are quenched as tow. Remember ye not the former things, neither consider the things of old. Behold, I will do a new thing," etc. This implies the ceasing of a great part of the rites of the ceremonial law, which were appointed on purpose to remember those events, [such] as the Passover, with all the rites and observances that have relation; the laws about the firstborn, both of man and beasts; and the feast of tabernacles, which was appointed in commemoration of the children of Israel's dwelling in tabernacles in the wilderness; [and] some ceremonies prescribed to him that offered the firstfruits. And there are many things in what is foretold concerning the ordinances of worship in the Messiah's days, in the nine last chapters of Ezekiel, that are inconsistent with the ceremonial law of Moses.[^007-note19]

153.[^007-note20] It was foretold that in the days of the Messiah's kingdom, instead of worship so much consisting in sacrifices of birds and beasts, and carnal ordinances and external performances, that worship should be appointed and maintained in the church of God that should be more simple and spiritual, consisting in prayer, praise, humility, brokenness of heart, obedience, almsgiving, etc. Is. 66:1-3, "where is the house ye build to me? . . . to this man will I look, even to him that is poor and of a contrite spirit, and trembleth at my word. He that killeth an ox is as if he slew a man; he that sacrificeth a lamb, as if he cut off a dog's neck; he that offereth an oblation, as if he offered swine's blood; he that burneth incense, as if he blessed an idol"; Ps. 50:7, etc., "Hear, O my people, . . . I will not reprove thee for thy sacrifices . . . I will take no bullock out of thy house, nor he goats out of thy folds. . . . If I were hungry, I would not tell thee: . . . Will I eat the flesh of bulls, or drink the blood of goats? Offer unto God thanksgiving; and pay thy vows unto the most High: and call upon me in the day of trouble: I will deliver thee, and thou shalt glorify me"; v. 23, "Whoso offereth praise glorifieth me: and to him that ordereth his conversation aright will I show the salvation of God"; Ps. 69:30-31, "I will praise the name of God with a song, I will magnify him with thanksgiving. This also shall please the Lord better than an ox or bullock that hath horns and hoofs"; Hos. 14:1-2, "O Israel, return thou unto the Lord thy God; for thou hast fallen by thine iniquity. Take with you words" (instead of sacrifices), "and turn to the Lord: say unto him, Take away all iniquity, and receive us graciously: so will we render the calves of our lips"; Ps. 65:1-2, "Praise waiteth for thee, O God in Zion: and unto thee shall the vow be performed: O thou that hearest prayer, unto thee shall all flesh come"; Is. 56:7, "Even them will [I] bring to my holy mountain, and make them joyful in my house of prayer: their burnt offerings and their sacrifices shall be accepted on mine altar; for mine house shall be called an house of prayer for all people"; Hos. 6:6, "For I desired mercy, and not sacrifice; and the knowledge of God more than burnt offerings"; Zech. 8:16-19, "These are the things that ye shall do; Speak ye every man the truth to his neighbor; execute the judgment of truth and peace in your gates: and let none of you imagine evil in your hearts against his neighbor; and love no false oath: for all these things do I hate, saith the Lord. And the word of the Lord of hosts came unto me, saying, Thus saith the Lord of hosts; The fast of the fourth month, and the fast of the fifth, and the fast of the seventh, and the fast of the tenth, shall be to the house of Judah joy and gladness, and cheerful feasts; therefore love the truth and peace."

154.[^007-note21] It is implicitly foretold that the Jewish sabbath should cease in the times of the Messiah's kingdom, because it appears in the institution of that sabbath, that the appointment of that sabbath was appointed to remember two things, viz. the creation of the heavens and the earth, and the redemption out of Egypt. But it is foretold by the prophets that in the Messiah's times, both these things should cease to be remembered or commemorated anymore. This is foretold of the redemption out of Egypt, in places taken notice of in the last section but one,[^007-note22] viz. Jer. 16:14-15, and Jer. 23:7, 9, and Is. 43:16-19. And this is foretold concerning the former, viz. the creation of heaven and earth, in Is. 65:17-19.

155.[^007-note23] Though it be foretold that the Jewish sabbath should be abolished, yet it is implied in the prophecies that a certain sabbath day should still be sanctified by the people of God, both Jews and Gentiles. This is particularly and expressly foretold in Is. 56[:1], where the abolishing of the ceremonial law is prophesied of in the same place, and a precept for the sanctifying of this sabbath joined with precepts of moral duties. "Thus saith the Lord, Keep ye judgment, and do justice: for my salvation is near to come, and my righteousness to be revealed. Blessed is the man that doth this, and the son of man" (if he be the son of man of whatever nation he be, whether he be the son of Jacob or no, or one legally pure or no) "that layeth hold on it; that keepeth the sabbath from polluting it, and keepeth his hand from doing any evil. . . . thus saith the Lord unto the eunuchs that keep my sabbaths, and choose those things that please me, and take hold of my covenant; even unto them will I give in mine house a place and a name . . . Also the sons of the stranger, that join themselves to the Lord, to serve him, and to love the name of the Lord, to be his servants, every one that keepeth the sabbath from polluting it, and taketh hold of my covenant; even them will I bring to mine holy mountain," etc.

Is. 58:12-14, "they that be of thee shall build the old wastes: . . . If thou turn away thy foot from the sabbath, from doing thy pleasure on my holy day; and call the sabbath a delight, the holy of the Lord, honorable; and shalt honor him, not doing thine own ways, nor finding thine own pleasure, nor speaking thine own words."

156.[^007-note24] That the new sabbath should be appointed to be kept in commemoration of that great and wonderful work of redemption that the Messiah should accomplish for the spiritual Jerusalem, instead of the old creation and redemption out of Egypt, agrees well with Is. 65:17-18, "For, behold, I create new heavens and a new earth: and the former shall not be remembered, nor come into mind. But be you glad and rejoice for ever" (as always remembering and keeping in mind) "in that which I create: for, behold, I create Jerusalem a rejoicing, and her people a joy." It has before been shown that by Jerusalem in such like prophecies, is meant the society or church of God's people, even all that are the subjects of the Messiah's redemption, §146.[^007-note25] Jer. 16:14-15, "they shall no more say, The Lord liveth, which brought up the children of Israel out of the land of Egypt; but, The Lord liveth, that brought them out of the north country," etc., intimating that they should now commemorate this new redemption, as they had done the old. So Jer. 23:7-8. So Is. 43:18-19--God, speaking of his great works of old in redeeming his people from Egypt, says, "Remember ye not the former things, neither consider the things of old. Behold, I will do a new thing"; as much as to say, "I do something else greater and more wonderful for you to remember."

157.[^007-note26] That the day wherein God restores and exalts the Messiah after his sufferings and humiliation, being therein rejected and despised of men, and set at nought by the chief men in Israel, the rulers and teachers in the Jewish Church: I say that that day should be the day which God would hallow and bless, and the day of which God (Is. 58) directs his people in the Messiah's time to call "a delight, the holy of the Lord and honorable" [v. 13], is exceeding agreeable with Ps. 118:22-24, "The stone which the builders refused is become the head stone of the corner. This is the Lord's doing; it is marvelous in our eyes. This is the day which the Lord hath made; let us be glad and rejoice in it."[^007-note27]

158.[^007-note28] It was foretold that the chief nations of the world for power, arts, wealth, merchandize and seafaring should be brought into the kingdom of the Messiah; and particularly that he should subdue and bring into subjection to him, the mightiest empire that should then be in the world. Ps. 22:28-29, "For the kingdom is the Lord's: and he is the governor among the nations. All they that be fat upon earth shall eat and worship"; Ps. 45:12, "And the daughter of Tyre shall be there with a gift; even the rich among the people shall intreat thy favor"; Ps. 72:10, "The kings of Tarshish and of the isles shall bring presents: the kings of Sheba and Seba shall offer gifts"; v. 15, "And he shall live, and to him shall be given of the gold of Sheba"; Ps. 89:24-25, "in my name shall his horn be exalted. I will set his hand also in the sea, and his right hand in the rivers"; Is. 42:10, "Sing unto the Lord a new song, and his praise from the end of the earth, ye[^007-note29] that go down to the sea, and all that is therein"; Is. 45:14, "Thus saith the Lord, The labor of Egypt, and merchandize of Ethiopia, and of the Sabeans, men of stature, shall come over unto thee, and they shall be thine"; Is. 60:5-6, "the abundance of the sea shall be converted unto thee, the forces of the Gentiles shall come unto thee. . . . all they from Sheba shall come: they shall bring gold and incense; and they shall show forth the praises of the Lord"; v. 9, "Surely the isles shall wait for me, and the ships of Tarshish first, to bring thy sons from far, their silver and their gold with them, unto the name of the Lord thy God, and to the Holy One of Israel, because he hath glorified thee"; v. 11, "that men may bring unto thee the forces of the Gentiles, and that their kings may be brought"; v. 13, "The glory of Lebanon shall come unto thee, . . . to beautify the place of my sanctuary; and I will make the place of my feet glorious"; vv. 16-17, "Thou shalt also suck the milk of the Gentiles, and shall suck the breasts of kings; . . . For brass I will bring gold, and for iron I will bring silver, and for wood brass, and for stones iron"; Is. 61:6, "ye shall eat the riches of the Gentiles, and in their glory shall ye boast yourselves"; Is. 66:12, "I will extend peace to her like a river, and the glory of the Gentiles like a flowing stream"; Jer. 31:7, "Thus saith the Lord; Sing with gladness for Jacob, and shout among the chief of the nations"; Mic. 4:3, "he shall judge among many people, and rebuke strong nations afar off"; Is. 53:12, "Therefore will I divide him a portion with the great, and he shall divide the spoil with the strong"; Zech. 8:22, "Yea, many people and strong nations shall come to seek the Lord of hosts in Jerusalem, and to pray before the Lord."

'Tis particularly foretold that the Messiah should conquer the mightiest heathen empire that should be in the world. Is. 25[:3], "Therefore shall the strong people glorify thee, the city of the terrible nations shall fear thee"; Is. 26:5-6, "For he bringeth down them that dwell on high; the lofty city, he layeth it low; he layeth low, even the ground; he bringeth it even to the dust. The foot shall tread it down, even the feet of the poor, and the steps of the needy"; Hag. 2:22, "And I will overthrow the throne of kingdoms, and I will destroy the strength of the kingdoms of the heathen."

159.[^007-note30] It was foretold that the Messiah in his kingdom, should be possessed of those parts of [the world] that had belonged to the four monarchies, and especially the last of them, viz. the Roman monarchy. Dan. 2:34-35, "Thou sawest till that a stone was cut out without hands, which smote the image upon his feet that were of iron and clay, and brake them to pieces. Then was the iron, the clay, the brass, the silver, and the gold, broken to pieces together, and became like the chaff of the summer threshing floors," etc.; vv. 44-45, "And in the days of those kings shall the God of heaven set up a kingdom, which shall never be destroyed: and the kingdom shall not be left to other people, but it shall break in pieces and consume all those kingdoms, and it shall stand for ever. Forasmuch as thou sawest that the stone was cut out of the mountains without hands," etc. So in the seventh chapter, the Prophet, relating the vision he had of the four monarchies, says, v. 9, "I beheld till the thrones were cast down, and the Ancient of days did sit." By these thrones must be especially understood the thrones of those forementioned monarchies. See also vv. 17-19, etc.,

These great beasts, which are four, are four kings, which shall arise out of the earth. But the saints of the most high shall take the kingdom, [. . .] Then I would know the truth of the fourth beast, . . . And of the ten horns . . . and of the little horn which made war with the saints,[^007-note31] and prevailed against them; until the Ancient of days came, and judgment was given to the saints of the most High; and the time came that the saints possessed the kingdom. Thus said he, The fourth beast shall be the fourth kingdom upon earth, which shall be diverse from all kingdoms, and shall devour the whole earth, and shall tread it down, and break it in pieces. . . . But the judgment shall sit, and they shall take away his kingdom, . . . And the kingdom and dominion, and the greatness of the kingdom under the whole heaven, shall be given to the people of the saints of the most High.

160.[^007-note32] That after Jesus' ascension, the Christian religion should be so propagated in the heathen world, in the primitive ages of the Christian church, as to subdue the whole Roman Empire, which, according to its ordinary limits, was extended from the river Euphrates, its boundary on the east, to the utmost ends of the old continent (which was till of late looked upon as the whole earth), even to the shores of the Atlantic Ocean, the western boundary of the Roman Empire, is remarkably agreeable to Zech. 9:9-10, "behold, thy King cometh unto thee: he is just, and having salvation; lowly, and riding upon an ass, and upon a colt the foal of an ass. . . . and he shall speak peace unto the heathen: and his dominion shall be from sea to sea, and from the river to the ends of the earth"; and Ps. 72:8, "He shall have dominion also from sea to sea, and from the river unto the ends of the earth." Solomon, who was a type of Christ, had his dominion in some sense from the river to the ends of the earth, as it was from the river Euphrates to the western limits of Asia at the shore of the Mediterranean Sea; but 'tis fit that the antitype should exceed the type, and that those prophecies should be fulfilled in a larger sense than Ps. 72:8 was fulfilled to Solomon: that whereas he reigned from the river Euphrates to the Mediterranean Sea, the Messiah should reign from the Euphrates to the ends of the earth in a larger sense, even to the Atlantic Ocean, so as to take in the isles of the Gentiles (as they were often called), agreeable to many prophecies, and, among others, v. 13 of this chapter.

161.[^007-note33] That the Messiah's kingdom should be propagated especially towards the north and west, as [has] come to pass with respect to the kingdom of Jesus Christ, was agreeable [to] Is. 49:6-7, "I will give thee for a light to the Gentiles, that thou might be my salvation to the end of the earth. . . . Kings shall see and arise, princes also shall worship," with v. 12, "Behold, these shall come from far: and lo, these from the north and from the west."

162.[^007-note34] It was abundantly foretold that the Lesser Asia and Europe, in particular, should be brought into subjection to the Messiah, and should belong to his kingdom, which were in the Old Testament commonly called by the name of "the isles," which appears as by other places. So by Gen. 10:5, speaking of the sons of Joseph, "By these were the isles of the Gentiles divided in their lands; every one after his tongue, after their families, in their nation." But that those isles should be brought into the kingdom of the Messiah, is very often foretold. Ps. 72:10, "The kings of Tarshish and of the isles shall bring presents"; Ps. 97:1, "The Lord reigneth; let the earth rejoice; let the multitude of the isles be glad thereof"; Is. 42:4, "the isles shall wait for his law"; Is. 42:10, "Sing unto the Lord a new song, and his praise from the end of the earth, ye that go down to the sea, and all that is therein; the isles, and the inhabitants thereof"; v. 12, "Let 'em give glory to the Lord, let them declare his praise in the islands"; Is. 51:5, "the isles shall wait upon me, and on my arm shall they trust"; Is. 60:9, "Surely the isles shall wait for me"; Is. 41:1, "Keep silence before me, O islands," etc.; Is. 49:1, "Listen, O isles, unto me; and hearken, ye people, from far";[^007-note35] Is. 66:19, "and I will send those that escape of them . . . to the isles afar off, . . . and they shall declare my glory among the Gentiles"; Zeph. 2:11, "The Lord will [. . .] famish all the gods of the earth; and men shall worship him, every one from his place, even all the isles of the heathen."

The Israelites of old were wont to call all countries and inhabited places beyond the seas, or with which the communication to and from the land of Canaan was wont to be only by sea, ["the isles"]. The chief of those countries were the Lesser Asia and the countries of Europe; indeed, those countries, all of them that were known to them, were peninsulas, as the Lesser Asia, Greece, Italy and Spain. But those that were properly islands (which are not excluded from the use of the word), all of them that were known to the Jews, were brought into the kingdom of Jesus Christ; as the isles of Cyprus and Crete, and the other isles in the Mediterranean Sea, and the innumerable islands in the Mediterranean Sea, and those great European islands, Ireland and Britain, the chief island in the world. And of later ages, the kingdom of Jesus has extended to America, a vast continent in the ends of the earth, far off upon the sea, divided from all the old world by a vast ocean.

163.[^007-note36] That very wild and barbarous nations have by Jesus Christ been instructed and civilized, and brought to the profession and practice of the true religion, is agreeable [to] Ps. 89:12, "The north and the south thou hast created them: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in thy name." Tabor and Hermon were haunts of wild beasts, as appears by Cant. 4:8, Ps. 42:6, Hos. 5:1. See Ps. 72:9, "They that dwell in the wilderness shall bow before him"; Is. 25:7, "And he will destroy in this mountain the face of the covering cast over all people, and the veil spread over all nations"; Is. 32:15-16, "and the wilderness became a fruitful field, . . . Then judgment shall dwell in the wilderness"; Is. 35:1-2, "The wilderness and the solitary place shall be glad for them; and the desert shall rejoice, and blossom as the rose. It shall blossom abundantly, and rejoice [. . .]: the glory of Lebanon shall be given unto it, the excellency of Carmel and Sharon, they shall see the glory of the Lord, and the excellency of our God"; vv. 6-7, "in the wilderness shall waters break out, and streams in the desert. And the parched ground shall become a pool, and the thirsty land springs of water: in the habitation of dragons, where each lay, shall be grass with reeds and rushes"; Is. 11:6, etc., "The wolf shall dwell with the lamb, and the leopard lie down with the kid," etc. So ch. 65:25; Is. 41:18-20, "I will make the wilderness a pool of water, and a dry land springs of water. I will plant in the wilderness the cedar, the shittah tree, and the myrtle, and the oil tree; I will set in the desert the fir tree, and the pine, and the box tree together"; Is. 42:7, "To open the blind eyes"; v. 11, "Let the wilderness and the cities thereof lift up their voice, the villages that Kedar doth inhabit: .et the inhabitants of the rock sing, let them shout from the top of the mountains"; Is. 43:19-20, "Behold, I will do a new thing; now it shall spring forth; shall ye not know it? I will even make a way in the wilderness, and rivers in the desert. The beasts of the field shall honor me, the dragons and the owls: because I give water in the wilderness, and rivers in the desert, to give drink to my people, my chosen." See Zeph. 2:11.[^007-note37]

164.[^007-note38] It was foretold concerning very many of the particular countries and regions of the earth that were afterwards converted to the Christian faith, that they should be brought to the true religion in the days of the Messiah's kingdom, as particularly:

EGYPT. Ps. 87:4, "I will make mention of Rahab . . . to them that know me"; Ps. 68:31, "Princes shall come out of Egypt"; Is. 19:18 to the end, "In that day shall five cities in the land of Egypt speak the language of Canaan, . . . and there shall be an altar to the Lord in the midst of the land of Egypt, . . . And the Lord shall be known to Egypt, and the Egyptians shall know the Lord . . . and the Egyptians shall serve with the Assyrians. . . . the Lord of hosts shall bless, saying, Blessed be Egypt my people"; Is. 45:14, "Thus saith the Lord, The labor of Egypt shall come over unto thee."

This is foretold of the nations of ARABIA, as *Kedar*, Is. 42:11, "Let the wilderness and the cities thereof lift up their voice, the villages that Kedar doth inhabit: let the inhabitants of the rock sing, let them shout from the top of the mountains"; Is. 60:7, "all the flocks of Kedar shall be gathered together unto thee." And *Nebaioth*, in the words next following: "the rams of Nebaioth shall minister unto thee: they shall come up with acceptance on mine altar."

MIDIAN and EPHAH. Is. 60:6, "The multitude of camels shall cover thee, the dromedaries of Midian and Ephah." And SHEBA, in the words next following: "All they from Sheba shall come: they shall bring gold and incense; and they shall show forth the praises of the Lord"; Ps. 72:15, "To him shall be given of the gold of Sheba." Both Sheba and Seba are mention[ed], Ps. 72:9-10, "They that dwell in the wilderness shall bow before him. The kings of Sheba and Seba shall offer gifts"; Is. 45:14, "Sabeans, men of stature, shall come over unto thee."

ETHIOPIA or CUSH, Ps. 68:31, "Ethiopia shall soon stretch out her hands to God"; [Ps. 87:4,] "behold [. . .] Ethiopia; this man was born there"; Is. 45:14, "The merchandize of Ethiopia shall come over to thee"; Zeph. 3:10, "From beyond to rivers of Ethiopia my suppliants," etc. It is said in the 68th Psalm that "Ethiopia shall soon stretch out her hands to God" [v. 31]. This was verified in the days of the gospel of Jesus, whether we understand it of Ethiopia in Africa or Arabia. If of the former, that soon stretched out her hands to God in the conversion of the eunuch, a man of great authority under Candace, the queen of Ethiopia [Acts 8:27], who doubtless had great influence on others of his country. Thus it was very soon that this Ethiopia stretched out her hands to God, this eunuch being the first Gentile that we have any account of that embraced Christianity after Christ's ascension. Or if we understand Ethiopia of Arabia, the gospel was very early carried thither; some of the first convert Jews in the day of Pentecost were inhabitants of Arabia, Acts 2:11. Arabia was the first Gentile country that the apostle Paul, the great Apostle of the Gentiles, preached the gospel in immediately after his conversion: he went from Damascus in Arabia, and seems to have spent about three years there, Gal. 1:17-18.

PHILISTIA. Ps. 60:8, "Philistia, triumph thou because of me"; Ps. 87:4, "Behold Philistia, [. . .] this man was born there"; Zech. 9:6-7, "And I will cut off the pride of the Philistines. And I will take away his blood out of his mouth, and his abominations from between his teeth: but he remaineth, even he, shall be for our God, and he shall be as governor in Judah, and Ekron as a Jebusite." In this country the gospel of Jesus Christ seems first to have been preached by Philip; see Acts 8:40.

TYRE. Ps. 45:12, "the daughter of Tyre shall be there with a gift"; Ps. 87:4, "behold, [. . .] Tyre, [. . .] this man was born there." We have an account, Acts 11:19, of the gospel's being very early carried into Phoenicia, of which Tyre was the chief city, although at first it was preached to Jews only; and we find an account, Acts 21:3, etc., of disciples at Tyre; and afterwards, all this country embraced Christianity.

BABYLONIA. Ps. 87:4, "I will make mention of Rehab and Babylon to them that know me." And ASSYRIA. Is. 19:23-25, "and the Egyptians shall serve with the Assyrians. In that day shall Israel be the third with Egypt and with Assyria, even a blessing in the midst of the land: whom the Lord of hosts shall bless, saying, Blessed be Egypt my people, and Assyria the work of my hands, and Israel mine inheritance." And PUL (whose posterity seemed to have settled in those parts, by so many names of the Assyrians and Babylonians having "Pul" in their composition, as Sir Isaac Newton observes in his *Chronology*).[^007-note39] And *LUD*, whose posterity seemed also to be settled in those eastern parts. Is. 66:19, "and I will send those that escape of them to the nations, [. . .] Pul, and Lud." The gospel was very early carried into those parts: for many of the Jews that were converted by the first outpouring of the Spirit, after Christ's ascension at Jerusalem, were dwellers in those parts of the world. Acts 2:9, "Parthians, and Medes, and Elamites, and the dwellers in Mesopotamia." And ecclesiastical history gives account of the Christian church being settled in those parts.

TUBAL. Is. 66:19, "and I will send those that escape of them to Tubal." Now Tubal was the father of the Asiatic Iberians, and the Tibareni. See "Scripture," no. 419.[^007-note40] But Christianity was propagated among those people. Thus Theodoret, in his 9th Book against the Greeks,[^007-note41] reckons among the converted Gentiles the Tibareni; and [in] his dialogue between an orthodox man and a Luciferian, among convert nations he mentions Iberians and Celtiberians; see Grotius, *de Verit*., Bk. 2, §18, margin.[^007-note42] The nations within the empire of Russia, seem to be chiefly of the posterity of Meshech and Tubal.

JAVAN. Is. 66:19, "I will send those that escape of them [. . .] to Tubal, and Javan, to the isles afar off, that have not heard my fame, neither have seen my glory; and they shall declare my glory among the Gentiles"; and again, Zech. 9:13, "When I have bent Judah for me, filled the bow with Ephraim, and raised up thy sons, O Zion, against thy sons, O Greece" (in the original, "O Javan"), "and made thee as the sword of a mighty man." The posterity of Javan settled there along on the southern coasts of the Lesser Asia, in the isle of Cyprus and Crete, and other isles of the Aegean Sea, and in Greece and Italy; see "Scripture," [no.] 419.[^007-note43] Those countries had the gospel preached to them by the Apostle and others that were the primitive Jewish converts, agreeable to those prophecies that speak of Javan as being subdued by the sons of Zion, the people of Judah and Ephraim; and that God would send to them such as should be of the remnant of the Jews, that should escape to declare his glory among them, and bring the people as an offering to the Lord: for when it is said, Is. 66:19, "I will send those that escape of them," the[^007-note44] word *them* is a relative that relates to those that are spoken in the beginning of the foregoing verse, where it is said, "I know their works and their thoughts"; and they are those spoken of in the two foregoing verses, which eat swine's flesh, etc., which doth primarily respect the Jews: the same that had been spoken of as God's enemies, that he would reject and testify his indignation again[st], in the preceding chapter, and in the former part of this chapter, and also in v. 14.

TARSHISH. Ps. 72:10, "The kings of Tarshish and the isles shall bring presents"; Is. 60:9, "Surely the isles shall wait for me, and the ships of Tarshish first, to bring my sons from far"; Is. 66:19, "I will send those that escape of them to Tarshish." Tarshish is spoken of as very early brought into the kingdom of [the] Messiah, that those that dwell there might be the instruments of bringing God's sons from [far]. The posterity of Tarshish were settled in Cilicia, the chief town of which was Cilicia; see "Scripture," no. 419.[^007-note45] Accordingly, Cilicia very early received the gospel, and Paul, a native of Tarsus, was made the chief instrument that ever was in the world of bringing God's sons from far, and his daughters from the ends of the earth. And Timothy, another great instrument of propagating the gospel, was born in those parts, his father being a Greek, though his mother was a Jewess, Acts 16:1. And by the account of the history of the Acts, the seaport places in the Mediterranean Sea were, generally speaking, first converted; but the whole Mediterranean Sea was called the Sea of Tarshish. 'Tis supposed also that Spain, or part of it at least, was settled by the posterity of Tarshish.

Besides those particular countries which the Jews of old had some knowledge [of], it was often foretold that the ends of the earth should be subject to the Messiah, and enjoy the privileges of his kingdom: by which ends of the earth we must understand especially to intend those parts of the earth that were beyond the then-known world, or beyond that part of the earth that was known to the Jews, in the extreme parts of the continent of that old world, or far off upon the sea. Is. 42:10, "Sing unto the Lord a new song, and his praise from the end of the earth, ye that go down to the sea, and all that is therein; the isles and the inhabitants thereof"; Is. 45:22, "Look unto me, and be ye saved, all the ends of the earth"; Ps. 22:27, "All the ends of the world shall remember and turn to the Lord"; Ps. 48:10, "According to thy name, so is thy praise unto the ends of the earth"; Ps. 65:5, "who art the confidence of all the ends of the earth, and of them that are afar off upon the sea"; v. 8, "They also that dwell in the uttermost parts are afraid at thy tokens: thou makest the outgoings of the morning and evening to rejoice"; Ps. 67:7, "all the ends of the earth shall fear him"; Ps. 98:3, "all the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God"; Ps. 2:8, "Ask of me, and I will give thee the heathen for thine inheritance, and the uttermost parts of the earth for thy possession"; Is. 49:6, "that thou mayest be my salvation to the end of the earth"; Is. 43:6, "bring my sons from far, and my daughters from the ends of the earth"; Is. 24:16, "From the uttermost parts of the earth have we heard songs, even glory to the righteous"; Is. 52:10, "all the ends of the earth shall see the salvation of our God"; Is. 62:11, "Behold, the Lord hath proclaimed unto the end of the world, Say ye to the daughter of Zion," etc.; Jer 16:19, "the Gentiles shall come unto thee from the ends of the earth, and shall say, Surely our fathers have inherited lies," etc.; Mic. 5:4, "now shall he be great unto the ends of the earth." It was foretold that God's glory should be made known to nations who, as they were beyond all the nations known to Israel, so were not known[^007-note46] to Israel, and were out of the hearing of the glorious works God had wrought towards that people. Is. 66:19, "I will send those that escape of them to Tarshish, Pul, and Lud, that draw the bow, to Tubal, and Javan, to the isles afar off, that have not heard my fame, neither seen my glory; and they shall declare my glory among the Gentiles."

These things are fulfilled in the propagating of the gospel to Italy, Spain, Gaul, Germany, Britain, Ireland, Poland, Denmark, Sweden, Norway, Lapland and Muscovy, Iceland, etc., and the West Indies and America.

165.[^007-note47] It was foretold that the common sort of people chiefly should be brought first to enjoy the benefits of the Messiah's kingdom, and afterwards persons in higher station. Zech. 12:7-8, "The Lord also shall save the tents of Judah first, that the glory of the house of David and the glory of the inhabitants of Jerusalem do not magnify themselves against Judah. . . . and he that is feeble among them at that day shall be as David; and the house of David shall be as God," etc.

166.[^007-note48] It was often foretold that the kingdom of the Messiah should be so propagated and extended as to reach thrones and palaces, and that kings and the greatest men of this world should be subject to him. Ps. 22:29, "All they that be fat upon earth shall eat and worship"; Ps. 45:9, "Kings' daughters were among thy honorable women"; Ps. 68:31, "Princes shall come out of Egypt"; Ps. 72:10-11, "The kings of Tarshish and of the isles shall bring presents: the kings of Sheba and Seba shall offer gifts. Yea, all kings shall fall down before him"; Ps. 138:4, "All the kings of the earth shall praise thee, O Lord, when they shall hear the words of thy mouth"; Is. 49:7, "Kings shall see and arise, princes also shall worship, because of the Lord that is faithful, and the Holy One of Israel, and he shall choose thee"; v. 23, "And kings shall be thy nursing fathers, and their queens thy nursing mothers: they shall bow down to thee with their face toward the earth, and lick up the dust of thy feet"; Is. 52:15, "kings shall shut their mouths at him"; Is. 60:3, "the Gentiles shall come to thy light, and kings to the brightness of thy rising"; v. 11, "that they may bring unto thee the forces of the Gentiles, and that their kings may be brought"; v. 16, "Thou shalt suck the breasts of kings"; Is. 62:2, "And the Gentiles shall see thy righteousness, and all kings thy glory"; Ps. 148:11, "Kings of the earth, and all people; princes, and all judges of the earth: . . . praise the Lord."

167.[^007-note49] 'Tis evident by the prophecies of the Old Testament, that after the Messiah should come, his people should for a long time be in afflicted, persecuted circumstances before that time of their peace, prosperity, dominion and glory, so often foretold, should come.

'Tis evident by very many predictions that the people of the Messiah should be greatly maligned by the men of the world, and should suffer very exceedingly, and be brought very low by their persecutions; and that by the Messiah, they should obtain a glorious conquest over their persecutors, and be delivered from them. This appears by Gen. 3:15 and 12:3; Deut. 32:36, 43; II Sam. 23:4, 6-7; Ps. 14:4-7, Ps. 22, Ps. 45:4, and 46:6-7, and 48:3, etc., and 50:20-21, and 53:3, etc., and 58, and 60, and 66:10-12, and 68:6, 13, 23, 30, and 72:4, 12-14, and 80:5-6, 12-13, and 85:1, and 86:14, and 89:10, and 97:10, and 108:6, 11-13, and 138:7 and 147:2-3, 6; Is. 8:9-10, and 9:4, and 11:4, 6, 8-11, 13-14, and 25:4-5, and 26:5-6, 16-18, and 27:1, 8, and 29:7-8, 19-21, and 30:29-31, and 31:4-5, and 32:7-8, and 33:1-3, and 35:3-4, 10, and 41:10-12, and 49:17, 24-26, and 51:7-8, 12-14, 17, 22-23, and 52:4-5, and 54:15-17, and 59:19-20, and 60:14-15, 18, and 61:1, 7, and 63:1, 5 to the end, and 64:10-12 and 66:5; Jer. 30:6-8, 10, 16-18, and 31:11, 15-16; Ezek. 20:35-37, and 34:20, etc., and 35, and 36:1-7, 15; Joel 3:1, 8; Obad., v. 10 to the end; Mic. 4:9 to the end and 7:8-11; Zeph. 3:12, 15 to the end; Zech. 9:8, 11-12, 15, and 12:2-3 and 14:12.

And it is evident from the prophecies that the people of the Messiah should be in a suffering, persecuted state after he should come. This is reasonable to suppose, from what the prophecies declare of the sufferings of the Messiah himself: if the prince shall suffer exceedingly, and be brought very low in his war with his own and his people's enemies, we must naturally suppose that the people suffer with him, as Moses and his people, and afterwards David and his followers suffered affliction together, and as a prince and people either prevail or suffer together in war; and not only is it reasonable to suppose this, but the prophecies do evidently represent that the people of [the] Messiah shall be made partakers of his sufferings from the hands of his enemies, and that in his victory and deliverance they shall be delivered and have victory. This is plainly supposed in that prophecy of the Messiah's sufferings and victory in the 22nd Psalm, in the 20th verse: "Deliver my soul from the sword; my darling from the power of the dog." It is supposed that in the deliverance of Christ's soul, his darling, or his church, is delivered from the power of the same cruel, persecuting foe; and so in what there follows, is set forth how his people that behaved with meekness under their suffering should rejoice, triumph and praise God with him. So when the great sufferings and deliverance of the Messiah are foretold in the 69th Psalm, his people, [which] are represented as being poor and prisoners with him, and bearing their sufferings in meekness, and crying to God for help, are delivered in his deliverance. Vv. 32-33, "The humble" (or "the meek") "shall see this, and be glad: and your heart shall live that seek God. For the Lord heareth the poor, and despiseth not his prisoners." So in the 89th Psalm, the Messiah, the prince of God's people, is represented as suffering, vv. 38 to the end: his crown cast to the ground, his hedges broken down, his strongholds brought to ruin, being spoiled by all that pass by, and being a reproach to his neighbors, his glory being made to cease, and his throne cast down to the ground, etc. And this his persecution, is represented as being the reproach and persecution of God's people, v. 50, "Remember, Lord, the reproach of thy servants; how do I bear in my bosom the reproach of all my neighbors?" And the matter is very plainly represented in the 102nd Psalm, as though Zion was persecuted with the Messiah her head, and suffered long in conformity to his sufferings: but at last, at the set time, has deliverance in a participation of the Messiah's deliverance. In the 11 first verses is livelily set forth the great sufferings of the Messiah from the cruelty of his enemies; and then, in the 12th and 13th verses, we have the antithesis: "But thou, O Lord, shalt endure for ever; and thy remembrance unto all generations. Thou shalt arise, and have mercy upon Zion: for the time to favor her, yea, the set time, is come"; which implies as much as this: "And though I suffer so much from the hands of my persecutors, and Zion, my darling, suffers with me, and in conformity to my persecutions, suffers very long, even through many generations, yet thou, her God, endurest unchangeable through all this long season; thou remainest through all generations; and though the time to favor Zion seem to be long delayed, yet there is a set time to favor, which will come at last." Then it follows in the next verse, "For thy servants take pleasure in her stones, and favor the dust thereof"; i. e. they take pleasure in her remains, though it be nothing but old rubbish stones and dust, the city having long lain waste. And then it is said, vv. 16-17, "When the Lord shall build up Zion, he shall appear in his glory. He will regard the prayer of the destitute, and not despise their prayer"; and vv. 19-20, "For the Lord looked down . . . to hear the groaning of the prisoner; and loose those that are appointed to death." That the church shall suffer with the Messiah, and be exalted in a participation of his exaltation, is also manifested by Zechariah, 3rd chapter: there, God, to encourage the Prophet with a prospect of a future glorious deliverance of his church from her enemies, exhibits to his view Joshua (or Jesus) standing before God, and Satan standing at his right hand to resist him; and the angel rebukes Satan, saying, "The Lord rebuke thee, O Satan; [. . .] is not this a brand plucked out of the fire?" And Joshua, as a type of Christ, has his filthy garments taken away, and is clothed in change of raiment, and having a fair or beautiful mitre set upon his head [vv. 1-5]. The Messiah and his people suffering together, and triumphing together, is compared to Moses' and his people's suffering and triumphing, in Zech. 10:10-11, "I will bring them also again out of the land of Egypt, . . . and will bring them again into the land of Gilead and Lebanon; . . . And he shall pass through the sea with affliction, . . . and the scepter of Egypt shall pass away."

The scattering of the people of the Messiah as a flock, and their being tried in the furnace of affliction, is very plainly spoken of as following the Messiah's sufferings, in Zech. 13:7-9. "Awake, O sword, against the man that is my fellow, saith the Lord of hosts: smite the shepherd, and the sheep shall be scattered: and I will turn mine hand upon the little ones. And it shall come to pass, that in all the land, saith the Lord, two parts therein shall be cut off and die; but the third shall be left therein. And I will bring the third part through the fire, and will refine them as silver is refined, and I will try them as gold is tried: and they shall call on my name, and I will hear them: I will say, It is my people: and they shall say, The Lord is my God."

In the 12th chapter of Daniel, the greatest time of the trouble and persecution of God's people of all that ever was, is represented as being after the appearance of Michael (or the Messiah), the great prince that standeth for God's people [v. 1]. And this their persecution, that should be after his coming, is represented in that chapter as of very long continuance.

Again, if we compare the prophecies of the Old Testament one with another, and with that history which is allowed by all, Christians, Jews and heathens, it may certainly be inferred that the people of the Messiah should be greatly persecuted long after he should appear in the world. It is foretold in the 9th of Daniel that it should be seventy weeks, or 490 years, from one of the edicts of the kings of Persia to restore and build Jerusalem after the captivity, unto the coming of the Messiah. And it is evident from history, that that time was out (let us reckon from which of the edicts we will), in the time of the fourth or Roman monarchy, while this monarchy was subject to one head or monarch, and that it continued many ages after that, before it came to be divided into ten kingdoms, as is foretold in that book of Daniel, chs. 2 and 7. But it is evident by the 7th of Daniel, that after this division of the fourth monarchy into ten kingdoms, there should arise an extraordinary power in the Roman Empire that should extraordinarily persecute the saints of the most High, and should continue to do so for a very long time, vv. 21 and 25.

168.[^007-note50] It was foretold in the prophecies of the Old Testament that there should appear an extraordinary and new kind of sovereign in the Roman Empire, that should arise after this fourth monarchy should be divided into many kingdoms, that should set up a form of government very diverse from all others, and very noted for his craft, cruelty, pride and boasting, and pretending to great things: pretending to a great superiority above other princes of the Roman Empire; whose boasts and high pretenses should be of a religious nature; assuming divine honor, encroaching on the prerogatives of the most High; making new laws in religious matters, as though he had power to disannul or dispense with the laws of the most High, and introduce new ones; that he should be the heart of the fourth monarchy under its last form of government; and that he should continue a long time, and that this sovereign should arise from very small beginnings, Dan. 7:8, 11, 20-22, 24-26. And it is evident that there has arisen just such a kind of sovereign as this in the fourth monarchy, since this monarchy has been broken into many sovereignties, concerning whom all these things are exactly verified.[^007-note51] Now it is foretold that this power should exceedingly persecute the saints of the most High, so as to wear them [out]. Doubtless, therefore, if we observe what people that is that have, in a peculiar manner and far above all others, [been] persecuted by this power, we may conclude that they are the true people of God. But the people that have been thus persecuted by this power, and that with such exceeding cruelty as no history can parallel, are those that are the professed followers of Jesus, that trust in him as the Messiah. Therefore, this people is the true people of God, and that Jesus they trust in, is the true Messiah: for this people is the people that are not only called the saints of the most High, but is the people that are represented as the people of the true Messiah in this prophecy, who shall with him triumph over this persecuting power, and should reign in great glory with the Messiah, vv. 13-14, 18, 21-22, 25-27.

169.[^007-note52] Though the prophecies are abundant in speaking of great and mighty opposition that should be made to the Messiah, and his people and kingdom, yet it is often foretold that his kingdom should forever continue after it is once set up, and that a profession of him should ever be upheld in the world. Ps. 72:5, "They shall fear thee as long as the sun and moon endure, throughout all generations"; v. 7, "In his days shall the righteous flourish; and abundance of peace so long as the moon endureth"; v. 15, "And he shall live, . . . prayer also shall be made for him continually; and daily shall he be praised"; v. 17, "His name shall endure for ever: his name shall be continued as long as the sun"; Ps. 45:17, "I will make thy name to be remembered in all generations: therefore shall the people praise thee for ever and ever"; Is. 9:6-7, "unto us a child is born, . . . Of the increase of his government and peace there shall be no end, upon the throne of David, and upon his kingdom, to order it, and to establish it with judgment and with justice from henceforth even for ever"; Ezek. 37:25, "my servant David shall be their prince for ever"; Ps. 110:1, "The Lord said unto my Lord, Sit thou on my right hand, till I make thine enemies thy footstool"; v. 4, "The Lord hath sworn, and will not repent, Thou art a priest for ever after the order of Melchizedek," i. e. "Thou art both a priest and king forever; Ps. 145:4, "One generation shall praise thy works to another, and shall declare thy mighty acts"; v. 13, "Thy kingdom is an everlasting kingdom, and thy dominion endureth throughout all generations"; Is. 54:15-17, "Behold, they shall surely gather together, but not by me: whosoever shall gather together against thee shall fall for thy sake. Behold, I have created the smith that bloweth the coals . . . No weapon that is formed against thee shall prosper; and every tongue that shall rise against thee in judgment thou shalt condemn"; ch. 55:13, "Instead of the thorn shall come up the fir tree, and instead of the brier shall come up the myrtle tree: and it shall be unto the Lord for a name, and an everlasting sign that shall not be cut off"; ch. 59:21, "As for me, this is my covenant with them, saith the Lord; My spirit that is upon thee, and my words which I have put in thy mouth, shall not depart out of thy mouth, nor out of the mouth of thy seed, nor out of the mouth of thy seed's seed from henceforth and for ever"; Is. 60:15, "I will make thee an eternal excellency, a joy of many generations"; v. 19, "the Lord shall be unto thee an everlasting light"; [v.] 21, "they shall inherit the land for ever"; ch. 66:9, "shall I cause to bring forth? [. . .] and shut the womb? saith thy God"; Jer. 33:17 to the end, "David shall never want a man to sit upon the throne of the house of Israel; neither shall the priests and Levites want a man," etc.; Dan. 2:44, "the God of heaven shall set up a kingdom, which shall never be destroyed: . . . it shall [. . .] consume all those kingdoms, and it shall stand for ever"; Dan. 7:14, "his dominion is an everlasting dominion, which shall not pass away, and his kingdom that which shall not be destroyed"; Joel 3:20, "But Judah shall dwell for ever, and Jerusalem from generation to generation."

170.[^007-note53] By the prophecies of the Old Testament, that state of glorious peace, triumph, honor, and prosperity and universal dominion to the church of God in the days of the Messiah, so abundantly insisted on by the prophets, should not be till after the destruction of that forementioned persecuting power that should arise in the latter times of the fourth monarchy.

See Dan. 7:5 to the end, with ch. 2:31 to the end and Dan. 12, throughout.

171.[^007-note54] By the prophecies, until the forementioned overthrow of their persecutors, and time of their glorious peace and prosperity following upon it, the people of the Messiah should chiefly be of the poorer sort of people. Is. 26:6, "The foot shalt tread it down, even the feet of the poor, and the steps of the needy"; ch. 29:19, "The meek also shall increase their joy in the Lord, and the poor among men shall rejoice in the Holy One of Israel"; and 41:17, "When the poor and needy seek water, and there is none, and their tongue faileth for thirst, I the Lord will hear them, I the God of Israel will not forsake them"; Zeph. 3:12, "I will also leave in the midst of thee an afflicted and poor people, and they shall trust in the name of the Lord"; Ps. 14:6, "You have shamed the counsel of the poor, because the Lord is his refuge"; Ps. 68:10, "thou hast prepared of thy goodness for the poor," with v. 13, "Though ye have lien among the pots, ye shall be as the wings of a dove covered with silver, and her feathers with yellow gold"; Ps. 72:4, "He shall judge the poor of the people, he shall save the children of the needy, and break in pieces the oppressor"; vv. 12-14, "he shall deliver the needy when he crieth; the poor also, and him that hath no helper. He shall spare the poor and needy, and shall save the souls of the needy. He shall redeem their souls from deceit and violence"; Is. 25:[3-]4, "Therefore shall the strong people glorify thee, the city of the terrible nations shall fear thee. For thou hast been a strength to the poor, a strength to the needy in his distress, a refuge from the storm, a shadow from the heat, when the blast of the terrible ones is as a storm against the wall."[^007-note55]

172.[^007-note56] The Messiah in the prophecies is represented not only as the Savior of God's people that should be then, after he should come into the world and set up his kingdom; but also the Redeemer of the saints in all ages, of those that lived many ages before his coming into the world, and even before the Mosaic dispensation. Job 19:25, "I know that my redeemer liveth," etc.; Ps. 110:1, "The Lord said unto my Lord"; Gen. 3:20, "And Adam called his wife's name Eve; because she is the mother of all living." When it is said, Is. 53[:6], "All we like sheep have gone astray; we have turned every one to his own way; and the Lord hath laid on him the iniquity of us all," it is most natural to understand it, so as to include the sins of the church of God in that day wherein the Prophet lived, that are very often spoken of by the Prophet, both in preceding and following parts of the prophecy. So God comforts Daniel, with respect to the many and great transgressions and iniquities of the church of the Jews that he had been confessing, with that, "Seventy weeks were determined upon thy people [. . .] to finish the transgression, and to make an end of sin, and to make reconciliation for iniquity" [Dan. 9:24]. And surely it is not natural to understand this exclusively of those sins of his and his people that then were, which he had been confessing and bewailing before, which was the very occasion of God's making this comfortable revelation to him. So the church of the Jews, when in great trouble, as being threatened with destruction in Ahaz's time, is comforted with that promise that "a virgin should conceive, and bear a son," etc., Is. 7[:14]; and this is mentioned to them as an evidence that they should be saved from the destruction they were now threatened with. So God's sending the Messiah into the world, is chiefly insisted on by the prophets Haggai and Zechariah to comfort the church of God at that day, with respect to the destruction they were threatened with from their numerous enemies that compassed 'em about. In the 3rd chapter of Zechariah, Joshua, that was then under Christ, the head of the church of the Jews, and is there spoken of as representing the whole church, is comforted with respect to the calamities that church was then subject to for their sins, and the destruction that they were threatened with, that God would forgive his iniquity, or cause it to pass from him, and so save him and his people from these calamities. V. 4, "And he answered and said to those that stood before him, saying, Take away the filthy garments from him. And unto him he said, Behold, I have caused thine iniquity to pass from thee, and I will clothe thee with change of raiment." And then the promise of the Messiah's coming and making full and perfect atonement for sin, is brought in as a confirmation and reason of this great benefit promised to Joshua and his people. Vv. 8-9, "Hear now, O Joshua the high priest, thou, and thy fellows that sit before thee: for they are men wondered at" (i. e. "Hear and believe this promise I have made of taking away your iniquities and those calamities that are the fruits of them, that make 'em a wonder to their neighbors): "for, behold, I will bring forth my servant the Branch. For behold the stone that I have laid before Joshua; upon one stone shall be seven eyes: behold, I will engrave the graving thereof, saith the Lord of hosts, and I will remove the iniquity of that land in one day." And in the next chapter, the Messiah, represented by this stone laid before Joshua as the foundation on which he should build the temple, having seven eyes graven on it: I say, the Messiah, represented by this stone, is here and in the next chapter represented as the Savior of that Jewish church that Joshua was then the head of, and even then building up. He is represented as the head or chief foundation stone of the temple Joshua was then building, in this 3rd chapter, 9th verse, and 4th chapter, 7th verse; and that church of the Jews is represented as being preserved and saved by these seven eyes that were graven on that stone, v. 10. And in the 6th chapter, the success and prosperity of Joshua in building of the temple, and restoring the church of the Jews, and the honor he and his fellows should receive, is represented as owing to the man whose name is the Branch. Vv. 10-12, "Take of them of the captivity, even of Heldai, and Tobijah, and of Jedaiah, which are come from Babylon, and come thou the same day, and go into the house of Josiah the son of Zephaniah; then take silver and gold, and make crowns, and set them upon the head of Joshua the son of Josedech, the high priest; and speak unto him, saying, Behold the man whose name is the Branch; and he shall grow up out of his place, and he shall build the temple of the Lord." And in Mal. 3:1,[^007-note57] the Messiah is promised to the Jewish church, thus: "the Lord, whom ye seek, shall suddenly come into his temple, even the messenger of the covenant, whom YE DELIGHT IN"; which, according to the idiom of the Old Testament, implies as much as if it had been said, "the messenger of the covenant, whom ye trust in as your defense and savior": for delighting and rejoicing in a person or thing, is often used in the Old Testament for trusting in them for salvation. So Ps. 22:8, "He trusted on the Lord that he would deliver him: let him deliver him, seeing he *delighted* in him"; here the word rendered "delighted," is the same as in Malachi. Ps. 37:3-5, "Trust in the Lord, and do good; [. . .] and verily [. . .] thou shalt be fed. DELIGHT thyself also in the Lord; and he shall give thee the desires of thine heart. Commit thy way unto the Lord; trust also in him; and he shall bring it to pass"; Is. 8:6, "Forasmuch as this people refuseth the waters of Shiloah that go softly, and rejoice in Rezin and Remaliah's son"; Ps. 63:7, "Because thou hast been my help, therefore in the shadow of thy wings will I rejoice"; Hab. 3:17-19, "Although the fig tree shall not blossom, . . . yet I will rejoice in the Lord, and joy in the God of my salvation. The Lord God is my strength."

173.[^007-note58] By the prophecies of the Old Testament, one fruit or attendant of the Messiah's coming and kingdom, should be the resurrection of the dead. Gen. 2:17, "in the day that thou eatest thereof thou shalt surely die," with ch. 3:15, "it shall bruise thy head," and vv. 19-20, "In the sweat of thy face shalt thou eat bread, till thou return unto the ground: for out of it wast thou taken: for dust thou art, and unto dust shalt thou return. And Adam called his wife's name Eve" (or "Life"); "because she was the mother of all living"; Job 19:25-27, "For I know that my redeemer liveth, and that he shall stand at the latter day upon the earth: and though after my skin worms destroy this body, yet in my flesh shall I see God: whom I shall see for myself, and mine eyes shall behold, and not another; though my reins be consumed within me"; Ps. 22:29, "all they that go down to the dust shall bow before him: and none can keep alive his own soul"; Ps. 68:18-20, "Thou hast ascended . . . thou hast received gifts for men: . . . Blessed be the Lord, who daily leadeth us with his benefits, even the God of our salvation. He that is our God is the God of salvation; and unto God the Lord belong the issues from death"; Is. 25:8, "He will swallow up death in victory; and the Lord will wipe away tears from off all faces," etc.; Is. 26:19, "Thy dead men shall live, together with my dead body shall they arise. Awake and sing, ye that dwell in the dust: for thy dew is as the dew of herbs, and the earth shall cast out the dead"; Is. 66:14, "And when ye see this, your heart shall rejoice, and your bones shall flourish as an herb" (compare this with the last cited place, and Ezek 37:1, etc.); Jer. 31:40, "And the whole valley of dead bodies, and of the ashes, . . . shall be holy unto the Lord"; Ezek. 37, concerning the resurrection of the dry bones; Dan. 12:2, "And many of them that sleep in the dust of the earth shall awake, some to everlasting life, and some to shame and everlasting contempt"; Hos. 6:2, "After two days will he revive us: in the third day he will raise us up, and we shall live in his sight"; Hos. 13:14, "I will ransom them from the power of the grave; I will redeem them from death: O death, I will be thy plagues; O grave, I will be thy destruction: repentance shall be hid from mine eye."[^007-note59]

174.[^007-note60] It is agreeable to the prophecies that the resurrection that should be at the Messiah's appearing and kingdom, should be of both good and bad of those that died in the ages preceding the Messiah's coming, as well as others. Dan. 12[:2], "some to everlasting life, and some to shame and everlasting contempt." Job speaks of his resurrection when the Messiah should appear, and his seeing him with his eyes (Job 19:25-27), though he lived even before the Mosaic administration. And Balaam, who lived in Moses' time and was a wicked man, speaks of his seeing the Messiah when he should appear, Num. 24:17, "I shall see him, but not now: I shall behold him, but not nigh: there shall come a star out of Jacob," etc.

175.[^007-note61] It was foretold in the ancient prophecies, that the Messiah should be the judge of the world. Ps. 50:3-6, "Our God shall come, and not keep silence: a fire shall devour before him, and it shall be very tempestuous round about him. He shall call to the heavens from above, and to the earth, that he may judge his people. Gather my saints together unto me; those that have made a covenant with me by sacrifice. And the heavens shall declare his righteousness: for God is judge himself"; Ps. 72:2, "He shall judge thy people with righteousness, and thy poor with judgment"; Ps. 96:10-13, "Say among the heathen that the Lord reigneth: the world also shall be established that it shall not be moved: he shall judge the people righteously. Let the heavens rejoice, and let the earth be glad; . . . before the Lord: for he cometh, to judge the earth: he shall judge the world with righteousness, and his people with truth"; Ps. 97:1, etc., "The Lord reigneth; let the earth rejoice; . . . Clouds and darkness are round about him: righteousness and judgment are the habitation of his throne. A fire goeth before him, and burneth up his enemies round about. His lightnings enlightened the world: the earth saw, and trembled. The hills melted like wax at the presence of the Lord, at the presence of the Lord of the whole earth. The heavens declare his righteousness, and all the people see his glory. . . . Zion heard, and was glad; and the daughters of Judah rejoiced because of thy judgments, O Lord"; Ps. 98:7-9, "Let the sea roar, and the fullness thereof; the world, and they that dwell therein. Let the floods clap their hands: let the hills be joyful together before the Lord; for he cometh to judge the earth: with righteousness shall he judge the world, and the people with equity"; Is. 2:4, "And he shall judge among the nations"; Is. 9:7, "upon the throne of David, and upon his kingdom, to order it, and to establish it with judgment and with justice"; Is. 28:16-17, "Behold, I lay in Zion for a foundation . . . Judgment also will I lay to the line, and righteousness to the plummet"; Is. 42:1, "he shall bring forth judgment to the Gentiles"; v. 3, "he shall bring forth judgment unto truth"; v. 4, "He shall not fail nor be discouraged, till he have set judgment in the earth"; Dan. 7:9, "I beheld till the thrones were cast down, and the Ancient of days did sit, whose garment was white as snow, and the hair of his head like the pure wool: his throne was like the fiery flame, and his wheels as burning fire. A fiery stream issued and came forth before him: thousand thousands ministered unto him, ten thousand times ten thousand stood before him"; vv. 13-14, "I saw in the night visions, and, behold, one like the Son of man came with clouds of heaven, . . . And there was given him dominion, and glory, and a kingdom, that all people," etc., with v. 22, "Until the Ancient of days came, and judgment was given to the saints of the most High," and v. 27, "And the kingdom and dominion . . . shall be given to the people of the saints of the most High"; Joel 3:2, "I will gather all nations, and will bring them down into the valley of Jehoshaphat, and will plead with them there for my people"; v. 12, "Let the heathen be wakened, and come up to the valley of Jehoshaphat: for there will I judge all the heathen round about"; [vv.] 14-16, "Multitudes, multitudes in the valley of decision: [. . .] The sun and the moon shall be darkened, and the stars shall withdraw their shining. The Lord shall roar out of Zion, and utter his voice from Jerusalem; and the heavens and the earth shall shake: but the Lord will be the hope of his people, and the strength of the children of Israel"; Mic. 4:3, "And he shall judge among many people, and rebuke strong nations afar off; and they shall beat their swords into plowshares," etc.; Mal. 3:1-2, "and the Lord, whom ye seek, shall suddenly come into his temple, even the messenger of the covenant, whom ye delight in: . . . But who may abide the day of his coming? and who shall stand when he appeareth? for he is like a refiner's fire, and like fullers' soap"; v. 5, "And I will come near to you to judgment; and I will be a swift witness against the sorcerers, and against the adulterers, and against false swearers, and against those that oppress the hireling in his wages, the widow, and the fatherless, and that turn aside the stranger from his right, and fear not me, saith the Lord of hosts"; and verse ultimate [Mal. 3:18] with ch. 4:1-2, "Then shall ye return, and discern between the righteous and the wicked, between him that serveth God and him that serveth him not. For, behold, the day cometh that shall burn as an oven; and all the proud, yea,[^007-note62] all that do wickedly, shall be stubble: and the day that cometh shall burn them up, saith the Lord of hosts, that it shall leave them neither root nor branch. But unto you that fear my name shall the Sun of righteousness arise with healing in his wings"; v. 5, "Behold, I will send you Elijah the prophet before that great and dreadful day of the Lord."

176.[^007-note63] It is agreeable to the prophecies of the Old Testament, that one great event appertaining to the administrations of the Messiah's kingdom, should be this visible world's coming to an end, or being brought to a total dissolution, and that this shall be at the time of his judging the world. Ps. 97:3-5, "A fire goeth before him, and burneth up his enemies round about. His lightnings enlightened the world: the earth saw, and trembled. The hills melted like wax at the presence of the Lord, at the presence of the Lord of the whole earth," with the context; Is. 2:21, "To go into the clefts of the rocks, and into the tops of the ragged rocks, for fear of the Lord, and for the glory of his majesty, when he ariseth to shake terribly the earth"; Is. 24:18-21, "the windows from on high are open, and the foundations of the earth do shake. The earth is utterly broken down, the earth is clean dissolved, the earth is moved exceedingly. The earth shall reel to and fro like a drunkard, and shall be removed like a cottage; and the transgression thereof shall be heavy upon it; and it shall fall, and not rise again. And it shall come to pass in that day, that the Lord shall punish the host of the high ones," etc.; Is. 34:1, "Come near, ye nations, to hear; and hearken, ye people: let the earth hear, and all that is therein; the world, and all things that come forth of it. For the indignation of the Lord is upon all nations"; [v. 4,] "And all the host of heaven shall be dissolved, and the heavens shall be rolled together as a scroll: and all their host shall fall down, as the leaf falleth off from the vine, and as[^007-note64] a falling fig from the fig tree"; Is. 64, at the beginning, "Oh that thou wouldst rend the heavens, that thou wouldest come down, that the mountains might flow down at thy presence"; ch. 65:17-18, "behold, I create new heavens and a new earth: and the former shall not be mentioned, nor come into mind. But . . . behold, I create Jerusalem a rejoicing, and her people a joy"; Joel 3:14-16, "Multitudes, multitudes in the valley of decision: for the day of the Lord is near in the valley of decision. The sun and the moon shall be darkened, and the stars shall withdraw their shining. The Lord shall roar out of Zion," etc.; Hag. 2:6, "Yet once, it is a little while, and I will shake the heavens, and the earth, the sea, and the dry land; and I will shake all nations," etc.; vv. 21-22, "I will shake the heavens and the earth; and I will overthrow the throne of kingdoms."

177.[^007-note65] It was foretold that the Messiah's kingdom, and the blessings of it, should be of longer continuance than heaven or earth. Ps. 102:25-28, "Of old hast thou laid the foundation of the earth: and the heavens are the work of thy hands. They shall perish, but thou shalt endure: yea, all of them shall wax old like a garment; as a vesture shalt thou change them, and they shall be changed. But thou art the same, and thy years shall have no end. The children of thy servants shall continue, and their seed shall be established before thee"; Is. 9:7, "Of the increase of his government and peace there shall be no end"; Is. 24:18 to the end, "the windows from on high are opened, and the foundations of the earth do shake. The earth is utterly broken down, the earth is clean dissolved," etc.; v. 23, "Then the moon shall be confounded, and the sun ashamed, when the Lord of hosts shall reign in Mt. Zion, and in Jerusalem, before his ancients gloriously"; Is. 45:17, "But Israel shall be saved in the Lord with an everlasting salvation: ye shall not be ashamed nor confounded world without end"; Is. 51:5-6, "My righteousness is near; my salvation is gone forth, and mine arm shall judge the people; the isles shall wait for me, and on mine arm shall they trust. Lift up your eyes to the heavens, and look upon the earth beneath: for the heavens shall vanish away like smoke, and the earth shall wax old like a garment, and they that dwell therein shall die in like manner: but my salvation shall be for ever, and my righteousness shall not be abolished," taken with the prophecy in the foregoing chapter [Is. 50], of which this is the continuation: there, the Messiah is spoken of very expressly, v. 6, "I gave my back to the smiters," etc.; Is. 54:7-10, "For a small moment have I forsaken thee; but with great mercies will I gather thee.
